國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑 ， 但仍維持高檔 ， 顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製 造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革 ， 也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出 。 部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利

⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。

⚫ 1-9 月房地產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%。

生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。

事件

品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index) 初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%， 長期通膨預期亦由3.4%升至 3.5%， 雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示 ， 此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔 ， 並維護國內產業利益 。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯 (Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免 ， 外媒亦將此次調整與11 月 3 日期中選舉連結 。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加 ， 使民眾傾向保守消費 。 若以產業區分 ， 歐元區服務業 、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁 ， 受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動 ， 出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品 ， 進一步擴散至房屋維護 、 公共運輸 、 住宿及外食等服務項目 ，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大 ， 亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐 。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%， 其中製造業 、 批發零售業年增率分別達5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能 ， 抵銷建築業年減4.4%等拖累因素影響 。 整體而言 ， 第三季GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致 ；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來 ， 儘管政府10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

用相關內容。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分 ， 對香港出口激增 121.1%、 對台灣 、 韓國出口增幅達96%， 對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定， 支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行 （BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息 。 央行指出 ， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧 、 高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展 ，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出 ，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

2026 年三季度货币政策执
行报告学习体会

货币政策趋稳   
2026 年三季度货币政策执行报告内容改动之处不多，我们认为，一定程度上意
味着货币政策中长期目标“建设金融强国”成为未来一段时间内的主要任务。 
 2026 年三季度货币政策执行报告中，相比二季度执行报告改动的内容不多： 
 一是对世界经济的判断，从“动能疲弱”变为“韧性不足”，以及出现了“主要经济
体经济表现分化” ， 对于通胀和货币政策 ， 二季度中提到的“全球通胀水平有所抬升，
主要经济体央行货币政策趋向调整”，也调整成了三季度的“通胀走势和货币政策调
整存在不确定性” 。 我们认为主要原因在于两方面 ， 一是2026 年带动全球经济增长的
人工智能科技发展，随着美国中期选举结果落定，开始遭受监管压力，二是推升2026
年通胀的国际能源价格，也有可能随着美国对外战略的调整，结束供给紧张的格局。
但是从全球范围内来看，一方面通胀有效回落需要时间，另一方面经济增长动力切换
不太容易，因此不排除在特定时间内，全球经济增长韧性面临“通胀处于高位、经济
增速下行、货币政策收紧”的三重考验。 
 二是在国内经济形势判断的部分，二季度的判断是“经济长期向好的内在支撑更加坚
实”，三季度则提出“仍面临供强需弱、结构分化、外部冲击等问题和挑战”。我们
认为这一提法对应的是我国经济增长表现出的两个特点，一是从内部来看，与全球经
济增长类似的 K 型分化，以电子行业为代表的人工智能产业链，对我国进出口、工业
增加值、工业企业利润数据都形成较明显的拉动，二是从外部影响看，2026 年前三个
季度三驾马车中净出口的累计同比贡献率逐步上升。因此无论是从产业链角度，或全
球化角度，我国经济都更容易受全球经济增长的影响。 
 三是关于增量政策和政策协同，二季度货币政策执行报告中提到的是“充分发挥各项
存量政策效能 ， 及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策” ， “加强与财政政策协同配合”，
“发挥好货币政策工具总量和结构双重功能”，三季度则是“发挥存量政策与增量政
策的集成效应”和“发挥好各类结构性货币政策工具作用”，三季度货币政策执行报
告中，增加了“‘六张网’建设”作为金融支持的重点领域之一。我们认为该部分内
容的调整，主要指向的是货币政策的“效能”而非一直以来货币政策宽松指向的“增
量”，可能较大程度上会降低市场对于货币政策持续宽松的预期。 
 四是货币政策改革更加具体，二季度货币政策执行报告中提到“推动贷款定价基准多
元化”，在三季度货币政策执行报告中已经具体为“推动贷款利率定价基准从比较单
一地参考 LPR 定价向更加多元的定价基准转型” ， 并且“目前已有参考 DR001 定价
的贷款陆续落地”。我们认为现阶段货币政策改革的目标不仅是降低实体经济融资成
本，同时也是加强货币政策传导的改革。 
 五是专栏的部分依旧是五个 ， 我们比较关注的是“专栏 3 设立债市非银大额赎回互换
便利”。“专栏 2 国际利率水平比较”也有提到，我国当前利率、利差，无论是与历
史水平相比还是与海外相比，都处于偏低水平，因此货币政策工具创设和使用的一个
重要考虑，都是“为经济稳定增长、高质量发展和金融市场平稳运行营造适宜的货币
金融环境”。 
 我们认为下一阶段货币政策的重要任务是聚焦完成中长期目标，也就是“金融强国”
建设。 
 三季度我国货币政策大事包括 ：7 月 24 日 ， 中国人民银行联合科技部等8 部门印发 《关
于加强科技金融领域数据开发利用的通知》 ；8 月 14 日，中国人民银行、国家外汇管
理局联合发布《关于跨国公司本外币跨境资金集中运营业务有关事宜的通知》；8 月
28 日，中国人民银行会同金融监管总局印发《关于改革完善房地产信贷管理 推动加
快构建房地产发展新模式的意见》；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行联合金融监管总局、中
国证监会、国家发展改革委、工业和信息化部、财政部、商务部、文化和旅游部印发
《关于金融支持服务业扩能提质的指导意见》 ；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行印发《关于
抵押补充贷款政策加力支持“六张网”建设有关事宜的通知》，下调抵押补充贷款利率
0.25 个百分点至 1.50%，将“六张网”建设纳入抵押补充贷款支持领域；9 月 28 日，中
国人民银行办公厅印发《关于加大科技创新和技术改造再贷款政策支持力度有关事宜
的通知》 ，增加科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度2000 亿元，并将该项再贷款支持比例
从 60%提高至 100%；9 月 28 日，中国人民银行决定增加支农支小再贷款额度 5000
亿元 ， 其中民营企业再贷款额度增加 3000 亿元 ， 激励引导地方法人金融机构扩大对涉
农、小微和民营企业的信贷投放；9 月 29 日，财政部会同中国人民银行、金融监管总
局印发《关于实施居民购房贷款贴息政策的通知》。此外，还有同部分国家续签双边
本币互换协议，以及任命海外人民币业务清算行事宜。 
 我们认为下一阶段货币政策可能采取对内“稳”对外“进”的方针：对国内经济和金融市
场以稳定为主，重点在于营造适宜经济高质量发展和平稳运行的货币金融环境，货币
政策方面，增量政策工具可能减少，政策重点在于发挥存量政策效能和加大现有货币
政策应用；对国外以推进人民币国际化、打造“人民币-人民币资产”循环为主，保持人
民币汇率弹性，不断增强人民币和人民币资产对国际资本的吸引力。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；全球经济增长动能减弱；地缘政治局势发展前景的不
确定性。

当前外部环境更加复杂多变， 世界经济增长韧性不足， 地缘冲突
和经贸摩擦多发频发，主要经济体经济表现分化，通胀走势和
货币政策调整存在不确定性。我国经济主要指标积极变化增多，
高质量发展取得新成效，但仍面临供强需弱、结构分化、外部
冲击等问题和挑战，既要坚定信心，保持高质量发展战略定力，
也要切实巩固拓展经济稳中向好态势。 
当前外部环境更加复杂多变，世界经济增长动能疲弱，地缘冲
突和经贸摩擦多发频发，供给冲击和输入型通胀压力影响仍在
持续，全球通胀水平有所抬升，主要经济体央行货币政策趋向
调整。我国经济高质量发展取得新成效，后劲持续增强，经济
长期向好的内在支撑更加坚实，制度优势和大国优势不断彰显，
要坚定信心，保持高质量发展战略定力，切实巩固拓展经济稳
中向好态势。 
坚持稳中求进工作总基调， 完整准确全面贯彻新发展理念，发挥
存量政策与增量政策的集成效应，加大逆周期调节力度，加力
扩大内需、 优化供给， 推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，努力完
成全年经济社会发展目标任务。坚定不移走中国特色金融发展
之路， 进一步深化金融改革和高水平对外开放， 加快建设金融强
国， 完善中央银行制度， 构建科学稳健的货币政策体系和覆盖全
面的宏观审慎管理体系，畅通货币政策传导机制。 
坚持稳中求进工作总基调， 完整准确全面贯彻新发展理念，充分
发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策 ，
加大逆周期调节力度，加力扩大内需、优化供给，推动经济持
续向新向优向好发展。坚定不移走中国特色金融发展之路，进
一步深化金融改革和高水平对外开放， 加快建设金融强国， 完善
中央银行制度， 构建科学稳健的货币政策体系和覆盖全面的宏观
审慎管理体系，畅通货币政策传导机制。

一
阶

工
作 
坚持实施好适度宽松的货币政策，为经济稳定增长、高质量发
展和金融市场平稳运行营造适宜的货币金融环境。 继续实施好适度宽松的货币政策。 
增强政策前瞻性灵活性针对性， 根据国内外经济金融形势和金融
市场运行情况，综合运用并适时调整货币政策工具，把握好政
策实施的力度、 节奏和时机， 保持流动性充裕和社会融资条件相
对宽松， 引导社会融资规模、 货币供应量增长同经济增长、 价格
总水平预期目标相匹配。 
增强政策前瞻性灵活性针对性， 根据国内外经济金融形势和金融
市场运行情况， 把握好政策实施的力度、 节奏和时机，加强与财
政政策协同配合，支持经济稳定增长、高质量发展和金融市场
平稳运行。 
综合运用并适时调整货币政策工具，保持流动性充裕和社会融
资条件相对宽松， 引导社会融资规模、 货币供应量增长同经济增
长、价格总水平预期目标相匹配。 
稳妥有序推动货币政策操作框架改革完善， 更好引导货币市场短
端利率围绕政策利率平稳运行。发挥市场利率定价自律机制作
用，加强利率政策执行和监督，推动贷款利率定价基准从比较
单一地参考贷款市场报价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价
基准转型， 持续深化明示企业贷款综合融资成本工作， 降低融资
中间费用，促进社会综合融资成本低位运行。 
平稳有序推动货币政策操作框架改革完善，更好引导货币市场
短端利率围绕政策利率平稳运行。发挥市场利率定价自律机制
作用，加强利率政策执行和监督，推动贷款定价基准多元化，
引导金融机构提高利率定价能力，持续深化明示企业贷款综合融
资成本工作，降低融资中间费用， 促进社会综合融资成本低位运
行。

发挥好各类结构性货币政策工具作用，不断完善工具设计和管
理，扎实做好金融“五篇大文章”，加强对扩大内需、科技创新、
中小微企业、“六张网”建设等重点领域的金融支持。持续做好
支持民营经济发展壮大的金融服务。 
发挥好货币政策工具总量和结构双重功能，落实好年初出台的
一系列货币金融政策，不断完善工具设计和管理，扎实做好金
融“五篇大文章”， 加强对扩大内需、 科技创新、 中小微企业等重
点领域的金融支持。

坚持以市场供求为基础、 参考一篮子货币进行调节、 有管理的浮
动汇率制度， 保持汇率弹性， 发挥汇率调节宏观经济和国际收支
自动稳定器功能， 综合施策， 增强外汇市场韧性， 稳定市场预期，
防范汇率超调风险， 保持人民币汇率在合理均衡水平上的基本稳
定。 
坚持以市场供求为基础、 参考一篮子货币进行调节、 有管理的浮
动汇率制度， 保持汇率弹性， 发挥汇率调节宏观经济和国际收支
自动稳定器功能， 综合施策， 增强外汇市场韧性， 稳定市场预期，
防范汇率超调风险， 保持人民币汇率在合理均衡水平上的基本稳
定。

拓展丰富中央银行宏观审慎和金融稳定功能，完善工具箱， 维护
金融市场平稳运行，持之以恒做好风险防范化解工作，坚决守
住不发生系统性金融风险的底线。 
拓展丰富中央银行宏观审慎和金融稳定功能，创新金融工具，维
护金融市场平稳运行，坚决守住不发生系统性金融风险的底线。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

M
Update
Broader recovery supports record earnings. 3Q revenue was NT$597.3bn (+1% Q/
Q, +82% Y/Y), with gross margin of 3.6% (+0.2ppt Q/Q, -0.1ppt Y/Y, 0.3ppt above
MSe), and net profit of NT$10.3bn (+6% Q/Q, +170% Y/Y, 42% above MSe). Data-
center revenue fell 5% Q/Q on shipment timing, while industrial, automotive and
Future Electronics revenue increased sequentially. The richer mix lifted gross margin
19bp Q/Q to 3.60%; operating margin rose 18bp to 2.38%, with operating expenses

increasing only 2% Q/Q. Net financing costs rose 22% Q/Q to NT$1.94bn.
AI drives a stronger 4Q. Management guides 4Q revenue of NT$730-770bn, with
the NT$750bn midpoint up 26% Q/Q, as data-center and communications growth
outweigh seasonal declines across consumer applications, industrial and automotive.
The guided gross-margin midpoint falls 36bp Q/Q to 3.24% on mix, but operating
profit rises 19% Q/Q to NT$16.96bn as expenses grow only 1%. 4Q EPS guidance is

NT$8.07–9.05, with the NT$8.56 midpoint up 7% Q/Q. Financing costs of NT$2.6–
2.8bn and the larger weighted-average share base temper the translation from
operating-profit growth into EPS.
Growth broadens, but cash conversion matters. Future Electronics grew over 40%
Y/Y in 3Q and has started shipping data-center products. Management remains
positive on 2027 AI demand, without providing a full-year earnings forecast.
Inventory days increased Q/Q from 47 to 66  and net debt from NT$27.6bn to NT

$46.4bn, although longer payables kept the cash-conversion cycle near 19 days
versus 18 in 2Q. We see further earnings potential from AI scale and Future’s
recovery, with funding requirements and inventory conversion the key execution
risks.

Valuation methodology
We cut our price target by 15% to  NT$296: We update our residual income model to
reflect earnings estimate revisions. We increase our equity risk premium assumption from
11.8% to 12.5% to reflect segment volatilities, and reduce our intermediate growth rate
assumption from 10% to 8% to reflect a more conservative medium-term growth forecast.
We keep other key RI assumptions unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, 0.9 beta, cash dividend

payout ratio of 85%, and a long-term growth rate of 3.0%. Bull case PT is cut from NT
$406 to NT$344.5, implying 10.6x 2027 P/E. Bear case PT is cut from NT$230 to NT$160,
implying 4.9x 2027 P/E. The risk reward is now skewed more towards the bear case due to
potential downstream demand disruption.
Exhibit 3: WT: Residual Income Model

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

- 1 - 
文曄(3036) 
26Q4資料中心恢復成長  
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 250 
前次投資建議 2026.8.6： 
「逢低買進」，目標價：$300

報告日期：2026.11.6 研 究 員：林秀泓

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 資料中心產品恢復強勁季增，通訊、資料中心產品受益 AI需求。 
➢ 2026 營收翻倍，2027營收增速減緩。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/5 收盤價： 195.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合
財測 QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，
YoY-0.14pt)，符合財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為產品組合
改善。EPS 7.97元。 
➢ 26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收
7,300~7,770 億元(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%)，主要由資料中
心出貨恢復帶動。毛利率 3.2%~3.3%。營業費用
7,154~7,546 億元。財測 EPS 8.07~9.05 元。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子受提前拉貨
影響，預期季減雙位數百分比；工業與儀器因季節性因
素，預期季減中個位數百分比；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需
求，預期季增高雙位數百分比；資料中心與伺服器預期季
增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約兩倍；消費性產品因提前
拉貨，預期季減中雙位數百分比；PC 預期季減；手機旺
季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙位數百分比。 
➢ 調整預估 2026/2027 年營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，
YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。 
➢ 目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的
低位。考量 2026 年 AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY
增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買進」 ，調整目標價
250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

營 運 分 析

文曄為 IC通路代理商 
文曄成立於 1993/12 成立，2002/08 上市掛牌，為提供全球專業電子零組件
通路服務的廠商，透過合併多家 IC通路商的方式持續擴大公司規模。營運總
部設立於台灣，目前在中國、韓國、新加坡、印度、泰國、馬來西亞、越南
等地區共有五十多個營運據點。代理半導體原廠超過 400 家，服務客戶超過
25,000 家。根據 2025 年 Gartner 最新數據，文曄已經成為全球最大和亞太
區最大的半導體通路商，市佔分別為 15.1%和 17.0%。 
產品組合方面，以代理產品的應用劃分營收，26Q3 汽車電子佔 5%、工業與
儀器佔 9%、通訊佔 13%、資料中心及伺服器佔 56%、消費性及其他佔 5%、
個人電腦與週邊佔 4%、手機佔 9%。

26Q3 財報符合財測，獲利再創單季新高 
26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合財測 5,720~6,120
億元(QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%)。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，YoY-0.14pt)，符合
財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為高毛利的工業、車用、Future 營收季增，產品組
合改善。營業費用QoQ+1.6%，費用率1.22%維持低檔，營業槓桿效益持續。
營業利益 142.3 億元，QoQ+9.5%，YoY+151.5%，優於財測中位數 140 億
元。業外方面，淨財務成本約19.4 億元，高於原先預估，因為資金成本上升。
其他業外收入 5.8 億元主要來自權益法認列日電貿的獲利，以及股利收入。
淨利 103.0 億元，QoQ+6.0%，YoY+169.9%，EPS 7.97元。

26Q4 資料中心恢復高雙位數季增，通訊持續成長 
26Q3 分產品線來看(以新台幣計)，汽車電子 QoQ+18%，優於前次法說預期
季減中低個位數百分比；工業與儀器 QoQ+13%，優於預期季增個位數百分
比；通訊 QoQ+5%，符合預期；資料中心與伺服器 QoQ-5%、YoY+114%，
符合預期；消費性產品 QoQ-7%，符合預期；PC週邊 QoQ持平，優於預期
季減個位數百分比，若不含記憶體則QoQ-11%、YoY-20%；手機QoQ+32%，
符合預期，受惠旺季與提前拉貨。 
展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子：受提前拉貨及庫存調整影響，
預期季減雙位數百分比，YoY+20%以上，全年YoY+30%以上；工業與儀器：
季節性因素，預期季減中個位數百分比，YoY+40%以上，全年成長約40%；
通訊：受益於 AI光通訊需求，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約一倍；
資料中心與伺服器：出貨節奏調整後恢復，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年
成長約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；消費性產品：因提前拉貨，預期季
減中雙位數百分比；PC：預期季減；手機：旺季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙
位數百分比。

半導體市場成長再度上修 
CSP 的 AI 資本支出展望正向，AI 需求強且具結構性。根據 Gartner 數據，
2026 年全球半導體市場由原先預估 YoY+92%再度上修到 YoY+108%，2027
年預估 YoY+39.2%(不含記憶體+33%)。半導體市場 2025~2029 年 CAGR
由 25.9%上修到 35.1%，2029 年市場規模預估達 2.7 兆美元。data center
為成長最強勁的領域，2026/2027 年預估 YoY+171%/+60%，2025~2029 年
CAGR+54.9%。 
文曄 26Q3 資料中心受出貨節奏影響，26Q4 恢復強勁季增，2026 全年成長
約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需求，成長動能
延續。Future 方面，歐美市場持續正向復甦，26Q3 營收 YoY+40%以上，車
用與歐洲工廠自動化已無去庫存壓力，資料中心業務也開始接單出貨。Future
較高毛利率可部分抵銷 AI 產品線低毛利率的影響。調整預估 2026/2027 年
營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，淨利 387 億元/450
億元，YoY+185.9%/+16.3%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。

目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的低位。考量 2026 年
AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY 增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買
進」 ，調整目標價 250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 2,000,000원 
현재주가 (10.21) 1,417,000원 
상승여력 41.1%

6개월 목표주가 2,000,000

현재주가 
(26.10.21) 
1,417,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

### Page 2

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

### Page 1

EMEA FX should remain supported by restrictive policy and
attractive carry, but performance will increasingly diverge. We
favour HUF, ZAR and TRY, while CZK remains supported and PLN
faces headwinds. Global yields, energy prices, ﬁscal risks and
geopolitics remain key drivers
Chris Turner, Rafal Benecki, Adam Antoniak, Mateusz Sutowicz,
Peter Virovacz, David Havrlant, Dmitry Dolgin, Valentin Tataru,
Stefan Posea, Muhammet Mercan and Frantisek Taborsky

Executive summary
Central banks across EMEA remain focused on currency stability as
inﬂation, ﬁscal risks and geopolitics keep the external environment
challenging. Restrictive monetary policy and attractive carry
support several currencies, particularly the forint, rand and lira,
while solid external balances and EU inﬂows provide additional
buﬀers. At the same time, high global yields, energy price risks,
ﬁscal concerns and geopolitical uncertainty should drive greater

diﬀerentiation across the region.
The CEE3 region, comprising the Czech Republic, Hungary and
Poland, should remain relatively well supported by restrictive
monetary policy, although the outlook is becoming more

### Page 2

diﬀerentiated. The Hungarian forint remains our preferred Central
European currency, with the National Bank of Hungary expected to
keep rates at 5.50% for the next 9-12 months, while ﬁscal
consolidation, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should keep the forint biased towards the stronger end of its 350-
370 EUR/HUF range. The Czech koruna should also beneﬁt from
positive nominal and real rate diﬀerentials after the Czech National
Bank raised rates to 4%, although weaker growth should prevent a

renewed hiking cycle. By contrast, we have turned mildly negative
on the Polish zloty. Narrower rate diﬀerentials, ﬁscal concerns and
rising political and rating risks should limit PLN performance,
despite strong economic growth and sizeable EU inﬂows. Overall,
global yields, energy prices and geopolitics are likely to remain
important drivers across the region.

In the managed currency segment in EMEA, the Romanian leu

should see greater stability after the volatility of the past two
years. Fiscal consolidation, diminishing rating risks and lower
government borrowing needs have improved Romania’s
fundamental story, while weak growth and falling inﬂation should
allow the National Bank of Romania to start cutting rates next year.
However, the central bank appears more willing to tolerate FX
volatility than in the past, suggesting that stability around current
EUR/RON levels should not be mistaken for a return to the

### Page 3

exceptionally tight management seen historically. Meanwhile, the
Central Bank of Turkey should continue its gradual easing cycle
while maintaining suﬃciently attractive real rates to contain
dollarisation and support reserves. Although disinﬂation has been
delayed and inﬂation risks remain tilted to the upside, renewed
reserve accumulation and contained FX demand suggest the
current exchange-rate regime remains ﬁrmly in place. We therefore
expect the lira to continue oﬀering attractive carry despite ongoing

nominal depreciation.
On the periphery of the EMEA region, South Africa’s rand should
remain well supported by a hawkish central bank and attractive
carry. With inﬂation pressures elevated, the South African Reserve
Bank is keeping policy ﬁrmly restrictive, while relatively low
volatility has reinforced the rand’s appeal. Progress on energy and
freight reforms could provide an additional tailwind in 2027,
particularly if the external environment improves, leaving the rand

among the stronger performers in our EMEA forecast. Israel’s shekel
faces a more balanced outlook. The Bank of Israel has responded to
low inﬂation and excessive currency strength with rate cuts and FX
intervention, signalling limited appetite for further appreciation.
Softer US tech performance could also weigh on investment and
hedging ﬂows. However, Israel’s persistent current account surplus,
strong reserve position and solid credit fundamentals should limit

depreciation, while any improvement in geopolitics would provide
renewed support.
Currency sections
EUR/PLN: Turning negative on the zloty
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/PLN 4.40 Mildly Bearish 4.39 4.36 4.34 4.33
We are shifting our view on the zloty from neutral to mildly
negative: The latest weakening of the zloty was driven mainly by global
factors: a hawkish Fed, high US Treasury yields, a stronger dollar and
concerns over French public ﬁnances. Rate hikes by both the Fed and

the ECB have also narrowed Poland's interest rate advantage, eroding
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

PLN’s carry appeal. Solid fundamentals should nonetheless prevent a
disorderly depreciation: Poland remains one of the fastest-growing EU
economies, its external position is sound, signiﬁcant inﬂows from the
Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) and the Security Action for
Europe (SAFE) defence programme are still ahead, and foreign
investors' exposure to Polish bonds is light. Moreover, the National Bank
of Poland (NBP) may tighten monetary policy in early 2027. However,

many of these factors are already priced in.
Risks to the zloty are primarily domestic and geopolitical: Several
downside risks for PLN have intensiﬁed. Public ﬁnances remain the key
vulnerability: the ﬁscal deﬁcit is the largest in the EU, public debt is
rising and approaching statutory thresholds, and no meaningful
consolidation is planned before the 2027 parliamentary elections.
Tense cohabitation between the government and the President limits

the scope for ﬁscal repair, while the escalating dispute over the
Constitutional Tribunal adds to the risk premium. Rating pressure is
mounting, with one rating downgrade already delivered this year and
the second outlook revised to negative. Geopolitically, high energy
prices linked to the Middle East conﬂict, escalation of the war in Ukraine
and hybrid attacks on Europe remain the main threats.
We expect EUR/PLN to remain in a 4.32-4.45 range over the coming

months: In the near term, risks are skewed towards a weaker zloty, as a
negative rating action or an escalation of domestic political tensions
could push the pair towards the upper bound. A gradual recovery of the
zloty in 2027 would require sustainably lower energy prices, lower
global yields, a ﬁrmer stance from the NBP, and continued EU-fund
inﬂows. Even then, loose ﬁscal policy ahead of the elections makes a
return to the stronger levels seen in the ﬁrst half of this year unlikely.

EUR/HUF: Rangebound, with a stronger-forint
bias
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
EUR/HUF 364.00 Neutral 360.00 365.00 365.00 36
The forint remains a prisoner of global forces: With the ﬁscal
consolidation plan presented and preparations for the EU’s Exchange
Rate Mechanism (ERM) II talks underway, there is no longer a single
domestic event that can be circled as the next major market trigger.
Instead, energy prices, geopolitics and global risk appetite are likely to

dominate. The forint also remains an important part of the National
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 5

Bank of Hungary’s (NBH) inﬂation strategy, especially given the high
degree of exchange rate pass-through. In this volatile environment, we
expect rates to remain on hold for the next 9–12 months at 5.50%.
Inﬂation may provide room for easing, but that room remains behind a
closed door. Only a sustained and structural improvement in the risk
environment is likely to provide the key.
EUR/HUF should remain contained within a 350-370 range: At the
stronger end, 350 represents a natural line in the sand. A signiﬁcant

decline in Hungary’s risk premium could take the pair towards this level,
but the accompanying dovish repricing would make a sustained break
lower diﬃcult. Markets are likely to bring forward expectations of rate
cuts, reﬂecting the Monetary Council’s underlying dovish bias, a view we
share. At the weaker end, the central bank’s readiness to use the
exchange rate channel should limit any disorderly sell-oﬀ. A non-linear
move towards or beyond 370 could trigger verbal intervention and, if

necessary, direct market action. We see an actual policy rate hike as a
last resort, with the central bank likely to reach for other interest rate
tools, including the FX swap rate, before touching the base rate.
Credible intervention does not always require the central bank to pull
the trigger; if markets believe the threat, they will do much of the
heavy lifting themselves.
Domestic factors should keep the forint on the stronger side: Without
a singular deﬁning event, markets will closely monitor monthly budget

execution, inﬂation developments, and progress in ﬁscal consolidation.
Actual EU-fund inﬂows, an expected return of the current account to
surplus and the possibility of positive sovereign rating actions should
keep the forint closer to the stronger end of its range. A gradual grind
lower in EUR/HUF could even bring 350 into view at moments of peak
optimism. However, such strength would probably be followed by
monetary easing, preventing a lasting appreciation beyond that level.

We therefore see the forint primarily as a tactical market within a
longer-term constructive story, supported by Hungary’s potential path
towards ERM II entry at the start of 2029.
EUR/CZK: Koruna holds up well in tough times
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/CZK 24.38 Neutral 24.30 24.26 24.22 24.19
This is not a hiking cycle: The Czech National Bank (CNB) increased the
policy rate to 4% in two steps yet stressed repeatedly that this doesn’t

### Page 6

represent the onset of another hiking cycle. Moreover, the tone of the
latest press conferences was relatively dovish, while the meeting with
analysts revealed that the country’s dwindling economic performance
is a growing concern for some board members. We saw few pushbacks
against the idea that the equilibrium rate is likely higher than generally
assumed. In turn, it seems we’re in the process of searching for the right
spot in a world of growing uncertainty. A weaker koruna contributing to

rising import prices will be one of the key factors that the CNB will
watch when it comes to maintaining price stability over the next year,
but interest rates should remain the policy tool of choice. Stable rates
are the most likely outcome if our forecast for next year’s average
inﬂation of 3.1% is right.
Constrained consumers: The second-round eﬀects linked to the
protracted Hormuz shock have started to materialise and will be felt in
renewed annual increases in regulated prices and food prices. Large

distributors are beginning to announce increases in energy and heating
prices, while higher water charges will also play their part. With food
prices also set to rise soon, there is little chance for any relaxation of
the monetary policy stance, unless household consumption and
economic performance prove weaker than expected. Indeed,
disappointing real GDP ﬁgures could put a lid on a potential hiking spree.
With real wage growth softening materially over the next year and

borrowing costs rising, many households would be forced to reconsider
their spending habits, especially when it comes to discretionary
spending.
Koruna enjoys rate support: Still, we will continue to see a positive
interest rate diﬀerential with the ECB in both nominal and real terms,
which makes the koruna attractive. The domestic currency is set to
remain stable in times of elevated uncertainty, while a gradual
strengthening trajectory is likely once the global turmoil shows signs of

settling down. Should oil prices recede further and remain lower for
longer, we would see fresh wind in koruna’s sails, as this would prepare
the ground for the Czech economic expansion to accelerate. Real
convergence would get back on track, and the more pronounced
outperformance relative to the eurozone’s real growth would once
again work in koruna’s favour. Moreover, the CNB is free to raise rates as
it pleases should domestic or imported inﬂation get out of hand; we

have some doubts about whether the ECB has the same room for
manoeuvre, as it must, like it or not, keep one eye on French bond
yields.
EUR/RON: No big changes expected ahead

### Page 7

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q2
EUR/RON 5.35 Neutral 5.35 5.35 5.40 5.45 5.45
Monetary policy should remain the main near-term anchor for the
leu: The National Bank of Romania (NBR) has continued to keep the
policy rate at 6.50% throughout 2026, and our baseline remains for
50bp of easing in 2027, pending a smoother disinﬂation process. Weak
activity and improving headline inﬂation would justify an early start,
but sticky price pressures on the services front, potential episodes of

political uncertainty and risk-oﬀ global events could delay the easing
cycle. In this context, we think that liquidity management will remain
an important part of NBR’s balancing act. The liquidity surplus fell
sharply through October, from around RON20.8bn to RON4.9bn,
supporting tighter money market conditions during the recent period of
intense FX pressures. However, FX-implied yields have since retreated
from an early-October peak to more manageable levels, broadly in line

with their one-year average. Acute pressure on the currency seems to
have eased since mid-October, although it’s not excluded that a smaller
liquidity surplus going forward will continue to support monetary
transmission and discourage renewed short positioning.
The domestic slowdown has yet to translate into a convincing
correction of Romania’s external imbalance: Retail sales have been
contracting for more than a year, reﬂecting weaker household demand,

ﬁscal consolidation and elevated inﬂation. Nevertheless, the January-
September trade deﬁcit remained visibly elevated at 6.3% of GDP in
2026, only marginally narrower than 6.5% in 2025 and 6.7% in 2024 for
the same period. The investment cycle has also sustained imports
despite weaker consumption. In particular, the ﬁnal push to complete
RRF-funded infrastructure, transport, energy and healthcare projects
has generated strong demand for imported machinery, equipment and

intermediate inputs. We think that Romania’s potential growth
currently sits at around 2.0%, but productive investment could lift it
towards 2.4-2.7% in 2027-2029 through better infrastructure,
connectivity and energy availability, which should favour the medium
and long-term fundamentals for RON.
Our baseline is for further controlled depreciation in 2027, with ﬁscal
credibility, political stability and capital ﬂows as key factors to
watch: Romania’s investment-grade rating preservation has provided

some relief, but the cautious approach of markets with regards to ﬁscal
implementation and the delivery of a credible medium-term
consolidation plan remains in place. In this context, uncertainties over

### Page 8

Romania’s capacity to absorb the remaining €6.4bn of RRF funds
represents an additional short-term risk. Delays or lower-than-expected
absorption would place greater pressure on domestic and market-
based funding. We think that the NBR will continue to maintain a rather
ﬁrm grip on the currency, discouraging one-sided positioning. We
expect EUR/RON to remain around 5.35 by end-2026 and move
gradually towards 5.45 by end-2027. Renewed episodes of political

instability, ﬁscal slippage or weaker RRF absorption could produce a
faster depreciation.
EUR/RSD: Stability is likely to prevail
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
EUR/RSD 118.00 Neutral 117.50 117.40 117.30 11
Active FX management should remain the main anchor for the
dinar: The National Bank of Serbia (NBS) surprised with a 25bp hike to
6.0% at the November meeting, seeking to contain second-round
inﬂation eﬀects from persistently high energy prices, while also leaning

against the recent depreciation pressure on the dinar. Our baseline
outlook forecasts no further hikes at this stage, and we expect 50bp of
rate cuts in 2027 (likely through the second half of the year), taking the
policy rate to 5.50% by end-2027. Nevertheless, we think that the
balance tilts more towards upside risks for rates, rather than to the
downside. Currency stability has been a key policy feature this year and
is likely set to remain an integral part of the policy framework ahead;

the NBS sold €475m between January and September, including
substantial intervention in September, when it sold €380m as energy-
related FX demand increased. In any case, reserves remain
comfortable, giving the central bank ample capacity to contain
volatility moving forwards.
A stronger domestic economy should support the dinar, although
ﬁscal stimulus and investment-related imports create external and
inﬂationary trade-oﬀs: Serbia’s GDP expanded by 4.0% year-on-year in

3Q26, accelerating from 3.8% in the second quarter and 3.2% in the
ﬁrst. Private consumption, a resilient labour market and the investment
programme ahead of EXPO 2027 should continue to support activity
ahead. However, the more expansionary ﬁscal stance, infrastructure
spending and the import-intensive nature of the investment cycle could
also act as catalysts for slightly stronger price pressures and trade
deﬁcits in the near term. That said, at around 43% of GDP, public debt

### Page 9

remains manageable, while Serbia’s macro fundamentals continue to
remain robust.
We expect EUR/RSD to remain broadly stable around 117.4-117.5
through 2027, although domestic politics and the unresolved NIS
transaction present near-term risks: The Student List won the 25
October parliamentary election, ending the previous governing bloc’s
longstanding dominance. While the result could support institutional
reform and closer EU alignment, uncertainty around the political

transition and future policy direction, alongside energy-related FX
demand, likely contributed to the dinar’s modest weakening towards
117.6. Meanwhile, 11 December is the target for completing
negotiations under which Hungarian oil group MOL would acquire the
Russian-controlled stake in Serbian oil company NIS, although a ﬁnal
purchase agreement and regulatory approvals remain outstanding.
With substantial reserves and a demonstrated commitment to FX

stability, the NBS should be able to absorb further temporary pressures
and keep the currency close to its desired range.
USD/UAH: Orderly hryvnia depreciation
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
USD/UAH 45.44 Neutral 45.20 45.30 45.40 45.50
Gradual UAH depreciation ahead: The hryvnia has weakened to record
lows against the US dollar. Even so, we expect further gradual
depreciation. Pressure stems from a ﬁrmer dollar on the back of a
hawkish Fed and renewed tension in the Middle East as well as

intensiﬁed Russian attacks on Ukraine's economy. The National Bank of
Ukraine (NBU) continues its strategy of "managed ﬂexibility": it
tolerates an orderly slide but intervenes heavily in the FX market to
prevent disorderly moves. Monetary policy has turned tighter, with a
series of rate hikes since the summer reaching 150bp in total
accompanied by guidance that rates will stay high well into 2027, as
inﬂation has turned into double-digit territory recently.

FX reserves have declined: External ﬁnancing remains the key anchor
for the hryvnia. However, international reserves have fallen markedly
from their record highs earlier in the year, as FX interventions and debt
service outpaced inﬂows. On one hand, disbursements under the EU's
Ukraine Support Loan have lagged behind plans because of delays in
agreed reforms, and Brussels has made further funding conditional on

### Page 10

their implementation. On the other hand, according to NBU data, the FX
interventions at the end of October were the highest in history.
Focus on peace negotiations: The macroeconomic outlook has
deteriorated signiﬁcantly because of the Russian war, but direct
technical peace talks between Ukraine and Russia with US participation
oﬀer some hope of an end to the war. Intensiﬁed attacks on industry,
energy and logistics, together with the blockade of seaports, have

pushed the economy back into contraction this year, while renewed
inﬂationary pressure is eroding real incomes. Labour shortages linked to
mobilisation and rising production costs continue to weigh on
competitiveness, and any recovery in 2027 is likely to be slow. Against
this backdrop, we expect USD/UAH to drift gradually higher, towards the
46.0 area in the coming months, at a pace the NBU appears willing to
accept as long as it remains orderly. Timely EU and IMF disbursements

are the main upside factor, while a ceaseﬁre with Russia, which would
change the picture materially, does not look imminent despite
intensiﬁed US diplomatic eﬀorts.
USD/KZT: Carry can cushion external risks
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3
USD/KZT 443.00 Bullish 450.00 440.00 450.00 46
High real rates should survive NBK easing cycle: The tenge enters 2027
with monetary policy still favouring portfolio inﬂows. The National Bank
of the Republic of Kazakhstan (NBK) started cutting rates in 2026 to

15.75% currently, as inﬂation moderated to 9.2% YoY and the currency
strengthened, but the policy stance remained cautious, with cuts
lagging the actual CPI slowdown. We expect the easing cycle to remain
gradual, leaving real rates high by regional and EM standards. This
matters increasingly for KZT as foreign participation in Kazakhstan's
local debt has been a key driver of the tenge's strength this year. The
main risk to our constructive view would be a faster easing cycle than

we expect, particularly if inﬂation proves stickier and reduces the real-
rate cushion. Conversely, sustained disinﬂation accompanied by
cautious rate cuts should preserve attractive carry and keep monetary
policy an important source of support for KZT.
Capital ﬂows are a new strength, but also a new sensitivity: One of
the biggest changes in the KZT story over the last 12 months has been
the growing role of foreign portfolio ﬂows. Progress towards Euroclear

connectivity, positive sovereign rating momentum, increased domestic
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies

### Page 11

debt issuance and high real yields have helped attract foreign investors
into KZT government bonds. At the same time, regular state-related FX
sales have fallen sharply. This makes the tenge more market-driven,
but also more exposed to changes in global rates and risk appetite.
After its strong rally, we see limited room for another large
appreciation. However, downside risks are also cushioned: a $2.2bn
backlog of National Fund-related FX sales accumulated between June

and October gives the authorities a potentially signiﬁcant supply buﬀer
should private inﬂows reverse. We therefore expect KZT to remain
resilient in 2027 even if global portfolio ﬂows become less supportive.
Resilience does not equal structural appreciation: Higher core yields or
a broader global risk-oﬀ episode, AI-led or otherwise, could weaken
foreign demand for KZT assets and put pressure on the currency. This is
increasingly relevant as carry-driven ﬂows become a bigger part of the

KZT story. But we would not expect an indiscriminate sell-oﬀ; still-high
real rates and the accumulated backlog of state FX sales provide
meaningful buﬀers against a reversal in private inﬂows, while the role of
oil should not be overestimated as export constraints, growing imports
and large dividend outﬂows prevented the tenge from beneﬁtting
materially from the recent oil price rally. Our constructive 2027 view
should therefore not be confused with a structurally bullish view on KZT.

Kazakhstan's persistent current account deﬁcit remains consistent with
gradual depreciation over the longer term. For 2027, however, we
expect domestic buﬀers to keep any depreciation contained even
under a less supportive global backdrop.
USD/UZS: Domestic buffers should keep the
soum resilient
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q
USD/UZS 11974.00 Neutral 11770.00 11828.00 1188
High real rates are here to stay: Uzbekistan enters 2027 with
monetary policy providing a substantial buﬀer against global volatility.

The Central Bank of Uzbekistan (CBU) kept its policy rate at 14.00% in
November and turned more cautious on inﬂation, raising its year-end
forecast to around 7% amid strong domestic demand, utility tariﬀ
increases and external food and energy risks. This makes near-term
easing unlikely, but we still see scope for cautious cuts in 2027 if ﬁscal
restraint continues and inﬂation pressures subside. Crucially for the
soum, easing should not require sacriﬁcing the sizeable real-rate

cushion. This should help retain carry support even if global rates

### Page 12

remain elevated. The main risk would be a combination of sticky
inﬂation and weaker FX, which would leave the central bank with less
room to cut and potentially require real rates to remain higher for
longer.
Gold export backlog provides an additional buﬀer if capital ﬂows
weaken: The soum's resilience this year is particularly notable because
it has received relatively little help from Uzbekistan's traditional source
of FX support. Only 18 tonnes of gold were exported in January-

September 2026, compared with 85 tonnes during the same period of
2025, despite much stronger gold prices. Instead, the soum's strength
appears to have been driven largely by capital inﬂows. That creates
both a vulnerability and a potential buﬀer for 2027. Higher global yields
or a broader risk-oﬀ episode could weaken carry-driven ﬂows, but a
recovery in physical gold shipments towards Uzbekistan's historical 80-
120 tonne annual range would bring additional FX supply into the

market. Combined with still-high real rates and continued reform-
related capital inﬂows, this should limit the soum's vulnerability to a
deterioration in global sentiment.
It is too early to consider the old depreciation trend defeated: We
therefore remain constructive on UZS for 2027, but like KZT, would
distinguish resilience from a permanently stronger currency. Continued
ﬁscal consolidation is helpful, with the deﬁcit having fallen from 4.7% of

GDP in 2023 to below 2% currently, but Uzbekistan continues to run
twin deﬁcits and somewhat elevated inﬂation risks. Higher core yields
or a global risk-oﬀ episode could still weaken carry support and put
depreciation pressure on UZS, although high real rates and catch-up
gold exports provide meaningful buﬀers against a large adjustment.
Over the longer term, we would therefore remain cautious about
extrapolating recent soum strength. For 2027, however, the

combination of restrictive real rates, ﬁscal restraint and potentially
stronger gold-related FX inﬂows should keep depreciation contained
even under a less supportive global backdrop.
USD/TRY: Controlled lira depreciation to
continue

USD/TRY 50.26 Bullish 51.50 54.30 57.10 59.80
Inﬂation path delayed in 2027: October inﬂation in Turkey came in
slightly above expectations, interrupting the recent downtrend. Even

### Page 13

so, we see annual inﬂation ﬁnishing the year below 30%. In the near
term, uncertainty around oil prices remains a key risk to the outlook.
Prolonged US-Iran tensions could quickly intensify price pressures,
particularly as the sliding scale mechanism continues to unwind. The
latest Medium-Term Programme raised the 2027 forecast to 21%, up
from 9% in the previous plan. Although revised higher, this projection
remains optimistic compared with the 23.3% expectation reﬂected in

the Market Participants Survey. The upward adjustment mainly reﬂects
both the carry-over eﬀect of stronger-than-anticipated inﬂation this
year on next year’s starting point and the inﬂuence of global economic
conditions. Overall, the new forecast suggests that the disinﬂation
process under the stabilisation programme launched in mid-2023 has
been pushed further out. We forecast 26% next year, with risks to the
upside.
CBT signals gradual easing: In its latest MPC statement, the Central

Bank of Turkey (CBT) adjusted its language, shifting attention from
“monetary policy decisions” to the scale and timing of future policy
actions. This change suggests that, unless the factors behind the
current easing cycle change materially, the Bank is likely to continue
lowering rates in December. From this perspective, October inﬂation is
unlikely to signiﬁcantly alter the policy outlook. The main risks for
policy remain developments in the Middle East and any deterioration in

the disinﬂation trend. At the same time, the Bank is expected to keep a
close watch on domestic demand, international reserve dynamics, and
depositor behaviour when assessing future policy moves. Maintaining
suﬃciently attractive real interest rates is likely to remain important in
limiting pressure on foreign exchange reserves. Against this backdrop,
we continue to expect the easing cycle to move forward gradually, with
the policy rate reaching 30% by end-2027.

Reserve accumulation resumes in early November: Investment fund
volatility has had only a limited eﬀect on dollarisation across deposits
and mutual funds, although lira-denominated funds experienced
outﬂows as some investors appeared to reduce money market fund
positions. At the same time, growth in TRY deposits and repo accounts
indicates that dollarization pressures have remained contained. After
accumulating more than $30bn through net foreign exchange

purchases between June and August, the CBT saw both its gross
reserves and net FX position excluding swaps decline in September and
October, largely reﬂecting turbulence in investment funds. However, the
Bank’s net FX position rose sharply in early November, pointing to a
return of non-resident inﬂows alongside subdued foreign currency
demand from residents. Additionally, although the CBT increased the
pace of FX depreciation lately, this appears to be a technical

USD/ZAR: Hawkish SARB and key reforms in
focus
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
USD/ZAR 16.66 Mildly Bearish 16.50 16.50 16.25 16
South African Reserve Bank stays hawkish: Having adopted a 3%
inﬂation target in 2025, the SARB has already hiked twice this year. With
fuel prices looking like they will stay high, the SARB is expecting

headline inﬂation to stay above 5% and is also concerned about
inﬂation expectations becoming unanchored. The risk of higher food
prices from El Niño drought conditions later this year is not helping
either. But even with growth contracting in the second quarter, the SARB
is clearly taking no chances with inﬂation. The market now expects
another 75bp of tightening (to 8.00%) over the next 12 months and
policy to remain ﬁrmly in restrictive territory. High implied yields and

relatively (by the rand’s standards) low volatility have made the rand
one of the better EMFX performers this year.
2027 will be a big year for reforms: Increasingly, we are hearing about
the need for legislated reforms to be implemented. These largely focus
on the energy and freight sectors. On the former, Eskom’s ability to
avoid further load-shedding, increase generation, improve its ﬁnances
and integrate the private sector will be key. On the latter, whether 11

private train companies can start using Transnet infrastructure to
dramatically increase freight volumes will be a key test. The good news
is that the Government for National Unity (GNU) seems to be ﬁnding a
way through thorny issues – e.g., ﬁnally passing the 2025 budget – and
the next national elections are not due until 2029. Consensus currently
expects South African growth in the 1.5-2.0% area.
Local assets have withstood external pressure well: Local asset

markets seem to have withstood the strong dollar and the US Treasury
sell-oﬀ relatively well. One-month USD/ZAR traded option prices have
been happy to trade at low, sub-10% levels since the summer. And
South Africa’s credit default swap remains relatively calm near +130bp,
perhaps because of the country’s primary ﬁscal surplus and a more
manageable current account deﬁcit of less than 2% of GDP.
Additionally, despite the ﬂows into South Africa last year after the SARB

adopted the 3% inﬂation target, foreigners only hold 25% of available

South African government bonds. This compares with a peak of 43%
back in 2018. Overall, we expect a hawkish SARB to limit the rand’s
downside, while some improvement in the external environment next
year, plus some progress on implementing key reforms, should allow
the rand to continue to perform well.
USD/ILS: Bank of Israel gets what it wants
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
USD/ILS 3.18 Mildly Bearish 3.15 3.15 3.05 3.05 3
Bank of Israel ﬁghts shekel strength: Having traded as low as 2.80 in

late May, USD/ILS looks to be heading into year-end well above 3.00.
Yes, the strong dollar has played a role here, but so has the Bank of
Israel (BoI). This year, it has cut rates by 100bp to 3.25% and also
intervened to buy USD/ILS back in May. The BoI’s dovish position has
been justiﬁed by CPI continuing to surprise on the downside. CPI has
been below the middle of the BoI’s target range (1-3%) all year. Yet we
suspect the BoI is more worried about the strong shekel from a growth

perspective. Here, the real, trade-weighted shekel remains 30% above
its 20-year average and will prove to be a headwind to Israeli exporters.
While Washington may want trading partners to step back from FX
intervention against local currency strength, it may see Israel as a
special case.
Shekel joined at the hip with tech: Part of the recent shekel weakness
may reﬂect weakness in the US tech sector. Strength there normally
helps the shekel via two channels: investment ﬂows and hedging. On

the former, a tougher IPO environment may well be weighing on ﬂows
into Israel. During good times, these ﬂows can be worth as much as
$5bn per quarter through the Israeli current account. On the latter, a big
fall in tech can see Israeli investors reduce their hedges on falling
overseas portfolios. For example, in the second quarter, Israeli pension
funds and insurers sold $18bn of FX against the shekel as they increased
FX hedges on booming tech portfolios. The reverse may be happening

now and leaves the shekel a little vulnerable if the US tech story has
further to correct.
Good-quality credit and current account surplus help: While the BoI
may not want USD/ILS to go too much below 3.00, that does not mean
USD/ILS needs to rally a lot either. Israel is seen as a solid A- sovereign
credit and has very strong FX reserve coverage of short-term debt and
imports. Its budget deﬁcit of 4-5% of GDP is seen as manageable, and

Document 372: FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Why is FX volatility so low? Even a 6% fall in the Nasdaq barely
budges USD/JPY these days. Have FX markets found a general
equilibrium, or is there some kind of separation between
geopolitics and macro? We think the answer lies more in the
structure of FX trading and do not rule out jump risk if the
shock is big enough

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Dmitry Dolgin
Whatever happened to FX volatility?
Despite wars in the Middle East and Continental Europe, an energy-
driven inﬂation spike and growing fears over an AI bubble, FX
volatility is close to its lowest levels in over a decade. If moves in
spot exchange rates are the ultimate driver of traded volatility, then
the recent stasis in spot USD/JPY in the face of a 6% drop in the
Nasdaq tells the story. In short, if exchange rates aren’t moving,
why should the market pay up for volatility?
It is not as though FX volatility is low in all markets. AI concerns
have seen equity volatility spike, while surging energy prices,
Your privacy is important

Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

central bank tightening, and fears about debt sustainability have
all seen interest rate volatility remain elevated.
FX volatility has remained muted
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The lack of spot FX movement or spot follow-through cannot
simply be blamed on some kind of separation between geopolitics
and macro. This year’s war in Iran has driven a massive hawkish
repricing at the short end of yield curves and turned easing cycles
on their heads. And in this outlook, we argue that if there is one

theme that FX markets should take seriously, it’s global monetary
policy cycles.
Any pickup in FX volatility more likely from
equities than bonds
One of our key calls for 2027 is that we don’t expect a signiﬁcant
pickup in FX volatility. Markets have built up considerable tolerance
for US policy and geopolitical headlines, and may be even more
willing to fade US political risk if a Democrat-led Congress and
weaker Republican support constrain US President Donald Trump’s

room for aggressive policy action.
If volatility does break higher, we think equities are a more likely
trigger than rates. Historical evidence supports that view, with
equity volatility consistently showing greater pass-through into FX
than rates volatility (chart below).

### Page 3

Sticky global inﬂation and prolonged monetary tightening are
more plausible risks than a major equity selloﬀ. Yet we don’t think
even a high-inﬂation scenario would be enough to recreate 2022-
2023 levels of FX volatility. Policy rates entered the Iran war from
much higher levels than at the end of the pandemic, while ﬁscal
support is smaller and labour markets are looser. The ingredients
for another 2022-23-style rates shock simply look less potent.

Greater FX shock potential from equities
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Structural factors: Growth in ﬁnancial
assets playing a role?
When thinking about factors that could be suppressing volatility in
spot exchange rates, we suspect the growth in the asset
management industry could be playing a role. Both assets under
management and cross-border positions have continued to surge
over recent years. Using World Bank and Bank for International
Settlements (BIS) data, we now estimate that global holdings of

equities and bonds are worth around 300% of global GDP. As the BIS
reported back in 2025, FX trading volumes were about 70 times the
volume of global trade in 2025, roughly double the ratio of 1992.
These ﬁnancial FX ﬂows, including portfolio hedging and
rebalancing, can be an important source of FX liquidity when they
are gradual and diversiﬁed. But correlated hedging or rebalancing

### Page 4

can amplify FX moves. The latter was the case during the Trump
tariﬀ shock in March/April 2025.
Stock of global equity and ﬁxed income
holdings
Source: World Bank, BIS, ING
Structural factors: FX engineering playing a
role?
One of the big trends in FX trading over recent years has been the
‘internalisation’ of FX ﬂows – the concept being that a bank can
deliver steadier, better pricing for customers by oﬀsetting client
ﬂow with other internal client ﬂow/hedging activity without having

to go to external liquidity venues.
We suspect there is quite a lot of overstatement of the extent of
‘internalisation’ out there, where banks may be including dealing
on ‘lit’ venues (transparent order books) as part of their
internalisation activity. Undoubtedly, the advent of non-bank
liquidity providers to lit venues has been a major contributor to the
depth of market pricing, as has the growth of algorithmic pricing
engines. The design of these algo pricing engines, which pull back

from putting large volumes through in thin conditions, has
probably contributed to calmer conditions, too. Indeed, it seems
that when volatility conditions pick up, the buy-side prefers to use
algos to execute rather than paying the wider spreads associated
with principal liquidity transfers.

But before we conclude that FX volatility is dead, evidence shows
that major events – Liberation Day in April 2025, or the start of the
Iran war in February 2026 – can still have a big say in FX markets.
Below, we show some data on the depth of the EUR/USD market
starting in 2025. The chart shows the impact on EUR/USD, in USD

pips, one minute after a trade has gone through. We show the
impact of four size buckets, equally weighted: $0-1m, $1-5m, $5-
10m and $10-30m.
Big events do still impact market depth, which could be due to
some non-banks pulling liquidity from lit venues.
So, while the above factors could be contributing to lower volatility,
our conclusion is that volatility is not dead and jump risk or fat tails
will still be a fact of life in FX markets.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher
International Data
There was no major data out yesterday.
Domestic Data
In Australia, the value of dwelling finance approvals surprised
materially to the downside in Q3, the 15.5% decline marking
the largest quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.

The decline looks to be mostly volume-driven, with the number
of loans falling sharply compared to the average size of loans.
Across segments, investor lending values dropped by nearly a
third (see here).

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya
